People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Sometimes I just... I spend hours replaying small things. Like if I bought an expensive tube of pigment, or... snapped at my spouse. I just sit there and... it makes me feel like I’ve ruined the whole household because of it.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over your life, were there distinct periods where you felt this way, followed by times where you felt completely like yourself again, or has this been one long, continuous stretch?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not continuous. Just... distinct times, I guess. If I had to add up all those gray, guilty periods where I lose interest in things... it's probably been about seven months in total.\n\nDoctor: During those gray, empty periods, have you noticed that you feel physically tired or lack the energy to go about your daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I still do what I need to do around the house. I don't think I feel... physically tired, just normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned having these 'gray' periods off and on; would you say you have had at least two separate, distinct episodes of feeling this way, with a period of feeling like your normal self in between them?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I haven't... I don't keep track of how many times it happens. Or what counts as normal in between. I'm just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you recall at least one specific time in the past where you felt this way, and then a clear period where you felt back to your normal self, before these feelings came back again?\n\nPatient: I... I really can't say for sure. I just... I don't remember it clearly enough to say if there was a specific time like that. It all kind of blurs together.\n\nDoctor: When you have these 'gray' periods, do they typically last for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don't know about two weeks at a time... I just know that if you add up all those gray times... the times I feel guilty and just don't care about anything... it's probably been about seven months total.\n\nDoctor: When you have these 'gray' periods, do they ever last for two weeks or longer at a time?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know. Like I said, I don't keep track of the days like that. I just know it adds up over time. I couldn't tell you if it's exactly two weeks straight.\n\nDoctor: If you think back to the longest time you felt that 'gray' way, did it ever go on for as long as two weeks without you feeling even a little bit like your normal self?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know about two weeks straight. I just... I know that if you add up all those gray times, the times I feel guilty and just don't care about anything... it's probably been about seven months total. That's all I know.\n\nDoctor: When you have these 'gray' periods, would you say you feel that way for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. When it gets like that... the house just feels gray and heavy. I'll just sit at the kitchen table feeling empty. Even if nothing bad actually happened that day. It just stays like that.\n\nDoctor: If you think back over the last few years, can you remember a time when you felt that 'gray' way for a while, then felt completely back to your normal self for a period, and then later felt that 'gray' way all over again?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. It's... hard to remember exactly how I felt a few years ago. It all just kind of blends together. I don't really pay attention to it like that.\n\nDoctor: Looking at the period of feeling 'gray' and empty that you are experiencing right now, has it lasted for at least two weeks without any significant breaks?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I guess maybe? I haven't really been keeping track of the days. It just feels like it's been going on for a while now.\n\nDoctor: During these current periods when you feel gray and empty, are you still able to carry out your usual daily responsibilities like work, household chores, or social activities, or do you find that you are unable to get these things done?\n\nPatient: I guess I haven't been getting much done. So... no, I can't really pick out a specific time for you.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
